--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed so that the two black nodes (F and C) are completely disconnected, we need to identify all paths between these two nodes and find the smallest set of edges whose removal will disconnect them.

1. **Identify immediate neighbors of the black nodes:**
   - Node F: Directly connected to E, B, and T.
   - Node C: Directly connected to N, R, O, G, and B.

2. **Trace paths between F and C:**
   - **Path 1:** F -> E -> B -> C
   - **Path 2:** F -> B -> C
   - **Path 3:** F -> E -> S -> N -> C
   - **Path 4:** F -> E -> S -> B -> C
   - **Path 5:** F -> T -> B -> C
   - **Path 6:** F -> T -> B -> S -> N -> C
   - **Path 7:** F -> B -> G -> C
   - **Path 8:** F -> B -> G -> J -> I -> H -> G -> C

3. **Identify critical edges:**
   - Removing the edge F -> B will disconnect the most immediate and simplest paths.
   - However, there are still multiple paths through other nodes.

4. **Determine the minimal cut set:**
   - Remove F -> B to break the most direct connections.
   - Remove E -> B to ensure F is disconnected from the central cluster involving C.
   - Remove B -> G to ensure no indirect paths from F to C through B.
   - Remove T -> B to ensure no redundant paths from F to C through B.

Through this analysis, the minimum number of edges to remove is 3:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(F, B), (E, B), (B, G)].
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 3, 'cut_set': [['N', 'B'], ['C', 'B'], ['C', 'G']]}

1. **Identify the black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are labeled F and I.

2. **Find the immediate neighbors of each black node:**
   - Node F is connected to nodes B and T.
   - Node I is connected to nodes H and Q.

3. **Identify the neighbors of the neighbors:**
   - Node B (neighbor of F) is connected to nodes A, E, S, G, and T.
   - Node T (neighbor of F) is connected to nodes B and H.
   - Node H (neighbor of I) is connected to nodes T and J.
   - Node Q (neighbor of I) is connected to nodes O and P.

4. **Find the shortest path:**
   - From F to B (1 edge)
   - From B to T (1 edge, connecting back to F's neighbor)
   - From T to H (1 edge)
   - From H to I (1 edge)
   - So, the path F -> B -> T -> H -> I involves 3 edges but T is also part of the path already covered by F.
   - Checking an alternative path:
     - F -> T -> H -> I involves only 2 edges.

So, the shortest paths can be either:
- F -> B -> G -> J -> H -> I (4 edges)
- F -> B -> T -> H -> I (3 edges)
- F -> T -> H -> I (2 edges)

The shortest path is clearly F -> T -> H -> I with 2 edges.

    - Node \( P \) is directly connected to nodes \( B \), \( E \), \( S \), \( F \), \( Q \), and \( J \).

2. **Identify Node \( R \) Neighbors:**
    - Node \( R \) is directly connected to nodes \( J \) and \( I \).

3. **Find Paths Between \( P \) and \( R \):**

    - **Path 1:** \( P \rightarrow J \rightarrow R \)
    - **Path 2:** \( P \rightarrow J \rightarrow I \rightarrow R \)
    - **Path 3:** \( P \rightarrow F \rightarrow T \rightarrow I \rightarrow R \)
    - **Path 4:** \( P \rightarrow F \rightarrow G \rightarrow T \rightarrow I \rightarrow R \)
    - **Path 5:** \( P \rightarrow E \rightarrow S \rightarrow T \rightarrow I \rightarrow R \)
    - **Path 6:** \( P \rightarrow E \rightarrow N \rightarrow O \rightarrow G \rightarrow H \rightarrow J \rightarrow R \)

4. **Determine Minimum Edges to Remove:**

    - To completely disconnect \( P \) and \( R \), the critical edges to remove are those that span across multiple paths.
    - The edges to consider removing are:
        - \( (J, R) \)
        - \( (J, H) \)
        - \( (G, T) \)

   Removing these three edges:
    - \( P \rightarrow J \rightarrow R \) is broken.
    - \( P \rightarrow J \rightarrow I \rightarrow R \) is broken.
    - \( P \rightarrow F \rightarrow T \rightarrow I \rightarrow R \) is broken.
    - \( P \rightarrow F \rightarrow G \rightarrow T \rightarrow I \rightarrow R \) is broken.
    - \( P \rightarrow E \rightarrow S \rightarrow T \rightarrow I \rightarrow R \) is broken
    - \( P \rightarrow E \rightarrow N \rightarrow O \rightarrow G \rightarrow H \rightarrow J \rightarrow R \) is broken.

    Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(J, R), (J, H), (G, T)].

1. **Identify the two black nodes**: Nodes H and Q.
2. **Find all paths between H and Q**: 
   - H is connected to J.
   - J is connected to I.
   - I is connected to P.

   - S is connected to N.

   - C is connected to R.
   - R is connected to O.
   - O is connected to Q.
   - This forms another path: H -> J -> G -> B -> E -> A -> S -> N -> C -> R -> O -> Q.

3. **Determine the edges that need to be removed**:
   - To disconnect H and Q, we need to cut all paths between them.
   - The simplest way is to remove edges that form a minimal cut set.
   - Removing edges (H, J) and (P, Q) disconnects the first path.
   - Removing either (R, O) or (B, E) would not suffice because there are other paths.
   - To ensure all paths are cut, we also need to remove edges (J, G) and (C, R).

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove is 4.

Image name: b+_task1_gt5_PAIR3
Task: task1_shortest_path_length
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: zero_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 15
--- Model Response ---
To determine the shortest path between the two black nodes, S and Q, we need to trace the connections through the graph.

1. **Node S** is connected to nodes: A, B, E, and F.
2. **Node Q** is connected to nodes: J, O, and P.

Next, we look at the paths from S to Q, starting from the directly connected nodes:

- **From S to A**:
  - A connects to E, B, and F. None of these directly connect to Q.
  
- **From S to B**:
  - B connects to C, E, F, and T. None of these directly connect to Q.

- **From S to E**:
  - E connects to A, B, and F. None of these directly connect to Q.

- **From S to F**:
  - F connects to B, E, T, and H.
  - T connects to F and B.
  - H connects to I and F. None of these directly connect to Q.
  
- **From S to B (revisited through other nodes)**:
  - B connects to G.
  - G connects to C, J, and H.
  - J connects to O, G, and I. J is connected to Q directly.

Thus, the shortest path from S to Q through B and J is:
- S -> B -> G -> J -> Q

This path has a length of 4.

However, we re-evaluate for a shorter path:

   - **Path 4:** E -> B -> C -> N -> O -> H -> I -> J
   - **Path 5:** E -> B -> C -> N -> O -> H -> Q -> J
